# ============================================================================
# RSBDEFS -- RS_Ballistics profiles.
#
# Any loaded package may ship an RSBDEFS lump. Every one is read, in load
# order, and a later profile with the same kind and name replaces an earlier
# one -- that is how a weapons or map package adds or overrides looks without
# code.
#
#     <kind> <name>
#       key = value, value, ...
#     end
#
# `#` starts a comment. Keys are case-insensitive. AN UNKNOWN KEY OR A BAD
# VALUE REFUSES THAT PROFILE, named with its lump and line; the other profiles
# still load.
#
# KINDS
#   style     a look the player picks in the menu: multipliers over everything
#   burst     one GPU particle emission -- the building block
#   (what textures are made of is the engine's: SURFACES.txt, not a kind here)
#   impact    what a landing round does, by surface
#   round      what a gun names: a pairing of ballistics and roundlook
#   ballistics how a round flies and hits (speed, radius, damage) -- the same on
#              every machine: no variants, no player setting
#   roundlook  how a round looks and sounds (sprite, glide, wake, impact, whiz)
#   wake       what a round sheds as it flies
#   flash     the muzzle: light, lit-air cone, bursts, flame, smoke
#   ejecta    spent casings and hulls
#
# A LOOK PACKAGE MUST ALWAYS BE ABLE TO SAY: THE ACTOR OWNS THIS.
#
# `damage = none`, `speed = none`, `radius = none` on a ballistics profile, and `kick = none` on a
# recoil profile. Each means the same thing: this profile is being named for its LOOK, and the value
# belongs to the weapon or the actor, not here.
#
# FOUR KEYS HAVE NEEDED THIS CURE IN TWO DAYS, so it is a property of the format and not a run of
# accidents. Without a hatch, naming a profile for how it LOOKS silently takes its gameplay too, and
# the mod that owns the actor loses a tie it never knew it was in. That is how a cartridge came to
# decide a gun's damage and a recoil profile came to decide where its bullets went.
#
# SO: ANY NEW KEY THAT REACHES THE SIMULATION -- damage, speed, size, spread, aim, range, count --
# GETS A `none` WHEN IT IS BORN, not after it ships. If the only thing a key can do is describe how
# something looks, it needs no hatch. If it can change what happens, it does.
#
# AND THE SAME IS TRUE OF A WHOLE KIND, which took two years and two lanes to notice. `flash none`
# has been legal since the first flamethrower and no other kind ever got its twin -- so the only
# way a gun could say "this genuinely throws no brass" was to name NO ejecta profile, which is
# byte-identical to forgetting one AND falls back to the house recipe besides. `recoil none`,
# `ejecta none` and `trail none` exist now. ANY KIND A GUN CAN NAME GETS ITS `none` WHEN THE KIND
# IS BORN.
#
# ABSENCE IS NOT A STATEMENT, and that is the whole of it. A missing recoilprofile is the house
# default. A missing ejectaprofile is whatever the default throws. An empty card reads as finished
# while a wrong card reads as wrong, which is exactly backwards -- the wrong one is the one you can
# see. This bit three different keys in one day, in both directions: a lane deleted a key to mean
# none on Tuesday and spent Friday explaining to me why deleting a key to mean none is wrong.
#
# A `none` THAT LOGS IS NOT A `none`. Naming `none` on an ejecta profile used to write an error
# into the log every map, because the lookup failed like any typo. A log that cries wolf every map
# is how you teach people to ignore the log -- the same failure as a check that cries wolf, only
# slower. So each `none` is short-circuited in code BEFORE the registry is consulted, and the
# block in this file exists so the NAME RESOLVES for a lint or a person grepping for it.
#
# OMITTING A KEY IS STILL AN ERROR. `none` is a stated intention; a missing line is a forgotten one;
# the file has to be able to tell them apart, and the refusal message exists to catch the second.
#
# VARIANT NAMES:  <base>[~style][.material][@tier]
#   impact bullet                   the default
#   impact bullet.metal             on metal
#   impact bullet@plain             at the plain tier
#   impact bullet~cinematic.metal   the cinematic style, on metal
# The most specific that exists is used. Material outranks style. A profile
# written for a tier is used as written; otherwise the tier scales counts
# (plain 0.4, normal 1, heavy 1.8, extreme 3).
#
# THIS FORMAT IS THE PROTOTYPE of the engine's effect-definition lump
# (Engine docs/ENGINE_SUPPORT_LIST.md): keep kinds and keys general.
#
# GPU PARTICLES. A burst with no `particle` is inline additive glow (sparks, chips,
# glints). A burst with `particle = <definition>` draws a PARTICLEDEFS definition,
# which can be lit, alpha-blended, soft and textured (dust, smoke, flame puffs).
# ============================================================================


# ---------------------------------------------------------------- STYLES
#   particles  glow  lights  marks  flash  cone  flame  smoke  whiz   (multipliers)

style gritty
  particles = 1.3
  glow      = 0.7
  lights    = 0.6
  marks     = 1.5
  flash     = 0.85
  cone      = 0.8
  flame     = 0.9
  smoke     = 1.8
end

style cinematic
  particles = 1.5
  glow      = 1.5
  lights    = 1.8
  marks     = 2.0
  flash     = 1.4
  cone      = 1.4
  flame     = 1.2
  smoke     = 1.5
  whiz      = 1.3
end

style clean
  particles = 0.6
  lights    = 0.5
  marks     = 0.5
  flash     = 0.7
  cone      = 0.6
  flame     = 0.8
  smoke     = 0.3
end


# ---------------------------------------------------------------- BURSTS
#   count       particles
#   cone        half-angle, degrees
#   speed       map units per second, jitter 0..1
#   life        seconds, jitter
#   color       r, g, b
#   glow        brightness (additive)
#   size        start, end (diameter, map units)
#   gravity     map units per second^2 (negative rises)
#   drag        1/s
#   orient      billboard | streak | flake
#   stretch     streak length, seconds of travel
#   aim         normal | reflect | back | along | up
#   offset      map units off the surface
#   shape       cone (the default) | disc: a disc spreads ACROSS its aim -- along
#               the surface, for aim normal -- lifted off it by up to `cone`
#               degrees (0-90). Only for bursts drawn with a `particle`.
#   particle    <PARTICLEDEFS definition>[, <another>, ...] -- several share the count, so
#               one burst throws chips of several shapes (the 3D chunks)

burst spark_hot            # sparks struck off stone: short textured streaks that arc and fall, cooling (rsb_spark_impact)
  count    = 10
  cone     = 55
  speed    = 320, 0.5
  life     = 0.35, 0.4
  aim      = reflect
  particle = rsb_spark_impact
end

burst spark_metal          # a ricochet off metal: long photoreal streaks, a hot head and a tail (rsb_spark_ricochet)
  count    = 16
  cone     = 50
  speed    = 460, 0.5
  life     = 0.45, 0.4
  aim      = reflect
  particle = rsb_spark_ricochet
end

# ---------------------------------------------------------------- FIXTURES
# WHICH TEXTURES ARE LAMPS. Not the same list as the `light` material in SURFACES.txt, which is
# tuned for the LOOK and is generous on purpose -- a bright ceiling tile deserves the pop and the
# bulb glass when it is shot. THIS list alone decides what DARKENS A ROOM when it dies, and it is
# strict: lit trim (LITEMET, LITESTON) pops and sparks without taking any light out, and an
# ordinary ceiling or floor that merely reads as bright is not a lamp at all.
# Anything can be a lit-looking surface; only a lamp is a fixture.
# Patterns are exact or end in * -- a PWAD adds its own names with one more line, no code change.
fixture lamp_wall
  textures = LITE2, LITE3, LITE4, LITE5, LITE96, LITEBLU*, LITERED, TEKLITE, TEKLITE2, BRICKLIT
end
fixture lamp_wall_tnt   # TNT's coloured lamps -- read out of the IWAD, and missing from the list above
  textures = LITEGRN1, LITERED1, LITERED2, LITEYEL1, LITEYEL2, LITEYEL3
end
fixture lamp_props   # LIT DECORATIONS: they carry their own light, so killing one snuffs it outright
  actors = TechLamp, TechLamp2, Column, BurningBarrel, HeadCandles, Candelabra
  actors = BlueTorch, GreenTorch, RedTorch, ShortBlueTorch, ShortGreenTorch, ShortRedTorch
end
fixture lamp_panel   # ceiling light panels: gridded on the hit, so a big one takes several shots
  textures = TLITE6_1, TLITE6_4, TLITE6_5, TLITE6_6, GRNLITE1
end

# ---------------------------------------------------------------- LAVA
# What a round does to molten rock. No splash and no wet: lumps thrown, a spit of hot droplets,
# embers lifting on the heat, and the air over it burning. A blast gets the heavier `_big` sets.
burst lava_globs           # lumps of molten rock thrown out, tumbling and cooling as they fly
  count    = 7
  cone     = 40
  speed    = 150, 0.6
  life     = 1.6, 0.4
  aim      = up
  particle = rsb_lava_glob
end

  count    = 22

  speed    = 260, 0.6
  life     = 2.2, 0.4
  aim      = up
  particle = rsb_lava_glob
end

# ---------------------------------------------------------------- MATERIALS
# What textures are made of is the ENGINE's now (surface materials, #16):
# RS_Ballistics' SURFACES.txt tags them and TexMan.GetSurface answers. The
# impact variants below (.metal, .wood, .glass, .liquid, .dirt) use those
# names; a surface another mod's SURFACES adds gets variants the same way.
# A `material` block here is refused.


# ---------------------------------------------------------------- HOTSPOTS
#   merge       radius -- a hit this close to a live spot feeds it (map units)
#   heat        per hit, lost a second, full -- the spot is at its strongest at full
#   bursts      <burst> <times a second at full heat>, ... -- emitted while it burns
#   light       radius, intensity        lightcolor  r, g, b
#   throb       period (tics, 0 = steady), depth (0..1) -- the light pulses on a beat
#   mark        shape, radius cold, radius hot, every (tics), life (tics) -- re-stamped as it grows
#   markcolor   r, g, b
#   shimmer     radius, strength -- a column of hot air every few tics
#   sound       <looping SNDINFO name>, volume at full heat | none
# An impact names one with `hotspot`: a beam or stream held on one place feeds ONE spot that
# heats up, instead of landing every hit on its own (no stutter, no buzz).

hotspot unmaker_burn       # a wall under the Unmaker: a molten spot that grows, glows, throbs with the beam, drips slag, stacks smoke and sizzles
  merge      = 24
  heat       = 0.05, 0.7, 1.6
  bursts     = unmaker_spatter 8, unmaker_slag 5, unmaker_smoke 12, laser_spray 3
  light      = 120, 3.0
  lightcolor = 255, 50, 28
  throb      = 5, 0.45
  mark       = pool, 3, 14, 6, 45
  markcolor  = 255, 70, 30
  shimmer    = 16, 1.2
  sound      = rsb/flame/pilot, 0.9
  smokevolume = 16, 0.8
  damage     = melt, 3, 12, 0.7, 0.06, 0.35
end

hotspot unmaker_burn_metal # metal under the Unmaker: brighter and sparkier, the molten mark hotter and wider
  merge      = 24
  heat       = 0.05, 0.6, 1.6
  bursts     = unmaker_spatter 12, spark_metal 3, unmaker_slag 6, unmaker_smoke 8, laser_spray 4
  light      = 130, 3.3
  lightcolor = 255, 60, 30
  throb      = 5, 0.45
  mark       = pool, 3, 16, 6, 60
  markcolor  = 255, 90, 40
  shimmer    = 16, 1.3
  sound      = rsb/flame/pilot, 1.0
  smokevolume = 16, 0.8
  damage     = melt, 3, 14, 0.6, 0.04, 0.45
end

hotspot unmaker_burn_wood  # wood under the Unmaker: it chars and smokes hard
  merge      = 24
  heat       = 0.05, 0.8, 1.6
  bursts     = unmaker_spatter 4, unmaker_smoke 18, dust_wood 3
  light      = 110, 2.6
  lightcolor = 255, 50, 25
  throb      = 5, 0.45
  mark       = pool, 3, 13, 6, 45
  markcolor  = 255, 60, 25
  shimmer    = 16, 1.1
  sound      = rsb/flame/pilot, 0.8
  smokevolume = 16, 0.8
  damage     = melt, 3, 12, 0.6, 0.15, 0.2
end

hotspot unmaker_burn_dirt  # dirt and rock under the Unmaker: melting to glassy slag, dust and smoke
  merge      = 24

  bursts     = unmaker_spatter 5, unmaker_slag 4, unmaker_smoke 12, dust_dirt 2

  damage     = melt, 3, 12, 0.8, 0.08, 0.35
end

hotspot unmaker_burn_glass # glass under the Unmaker: shards and molten spatter, a small bright mark
  merge      = 24
  heat       = 0.05, 0.8, 1.6
  bursts     = unmaker_spatter 7, glint_glass 2, unmaker_smoke 6
  light      = 120, 3.0
  lightcolor = 255, 56, 30
  throb      = 5, 0.45

  smokevolume = 16, 0.8
  damage     = melt, 2, 8, 0.4, 0.02, 0.4
end

hotspot unmaker_burn_liquid # water under the Unmaker: boiling, steam pouring off, no mark
  merge      = 28

  bursts     = flesh_cook_steam 14, splash_liquid 3

  lightcolor = 255, 50, 30

end

# ---------------------------------------------------------------- IMPACTS
#   bursts      burst names, or none
#   mark        shape, radius (map units), life (tics)
#               shapes: pool bar gouge ring hexfield hexring spiral boxring
#                       star sunburst grid invert box
#   markcolor   r, g, b
#               A mark GLOWS, so only a hot hit leaves one (owner: only hot things
#               glow): metal a round strikes, energy, blasts, burns. Never stone,
#               wood or dirt; their holes wait for the engine's wall damage (#17).
#   sound       SNDINFO name | none
#   light       radius, intensity, tics      lightcolor  r, g, b
#   glance      degrees from the surface, sound, burst -- a ricochet
#   vary        count <0..0.9>, size <..>, light <..> -- each hit that part comes out up
#               to this much bigger or smaller (hashed per hit)
#   maybe       <burst> <chance 0..1>, ... -- bursts only some hits throw
#   hotspot     <hotspot profile> -- each hit feeds that hotspot instead of landing on its own:
#               for a beam or stream held on one place (see HOTSPOTS)
#   heat        radius, strength, tics -- heat shimmer where it lands, fading (the
#               engine's; turned on in Ballistics & Effects, Heat shimmer)
#   scale       count, size -- every burst's particle count and size times these:
#               how hard a class of round bites (a pistol a little, a rifle a lot,
#               one pellet of buckshot little). 1, 1 when not given

impact bullet
  bursts    = spark_hot, chips_concrete, dust_concrete
  sound     = rsb/impact/concrete
  glance    = 18, rsb/ricochet, spark_hot
  damage     = hole_chip, 2.2, 0.5, 0.25, 0
  glancedamage = gouge, 3.08, 0.3, 0.25, 0, travel
end

impact bullet@plain
  bursts    = spark_hot
  scale     = 2.5, 1       # the plain level scales counts by 0.4: 2.5 keeps the ten sparks it always threw
  sound     = rsb/impact/concrete
end

# ---- IMPACTS BY WEAPON CLASS. A round look names its class's impact; the `bullet`
# family above stays for anything that names it. Each class lists every material,
# because a variant replaces its base. `scale` sets how hard the class bites; buckshot
# is per pellet, with no ricochet sound or light per pellet.

# buckshot -- one pellet of buckshot: small, so a blast peppers a wall
# Buckshot: seven pellets a shot, each its own -- only some spark or jet dust back out,
# so the wall takes one ragged blast, not seven identical stamps.
# THE GROUND KICK (a flash's `groundkick`): a big gun's blast raising the floor under it.
# Silent -- the gun is the sound -- with no mark and no light.
impact kick_blast
  bursts    = dust_kick_ring
  vary      = count 0.4, size 0.3
  sound     = none
end

impact kick_blast.wood
  bursts    = dust_kick_ring_wood
  vary      = count 0.4, size 0.3
  sound     = none
end

  sound     = none

# ---- the rest of the arsenal: plasma, rockets, the BFG (projectiles.zs), and the
# rail and saw puffs (RSB_RailPuff, RSB_SawPuff). Their own explosion sprites and
# sounds stay; these add light, particles, smoke and marks.
burst plasma_splash
  count   = 22
  cone    = 70
  speed   = 260, 0.5
  life    = 0.3, 0.4
  color   = 120, 190, 255
  glow    = 3.0
  size    = 0.9, 0.2
  gravity = 150
  drag    = 2.5
  orient  = streak
  stretch = 0.02
  aim     = reflect
end

  count   = 10
  cone    = 60
  speed   = 60, 0.6
  life    = 0.6, 0.4
  color   = 150, 210, 255
  glow    = 2.0
  size    = 2.5, 0.5
  drag    = 2.0
end

# THE CHAINSAWS: the chain bites about nine times a second while the bar is held on a surface, so each bite
# feeds a hotspot that builds one continuous cut; a bite only now and then throws something of its own.
# The saw's own engine and hit sounds are the gun's. Bodies it cuts bleed: that is their own mod's.

hotspot saw_cut_stone         # a chain held on stone: grit sparks fanned along the cut, specks, dust blown along the wall, chips; no glow (stone is not hot)
  merge      = 16
  heat       = 0.3, 1.2, 1.0
  bursts     = saw_sparks_stone 14, saw_grit 6, saw_dust 12, chips_concrete 2
  light      = 60, 1.4
  lightcolor = 255, 190, 120
  sound      = none
  smokevolume = 14, 0.6
  damage     = cut, 3, 6, 0.8, 0.03, 0, travel
end

hotspot saw_cut_metal         # a chain held on metal: a rooster tail raked along the face, white-hot steel flecks, a gouge that glows hot, burnt-oil smoke, the air bending over it
  merge      = 16
  heat       = 0.3, 1.2, 1.0
  bursts     = saw_sparks_metal 20, saw_sparkler_metal 3, spark_spray 6, ember_metal 4, saw_smoke_metal 3
  light      = 80, 1.8
  lightcolor = 255, 196, 130
  mark       = gouge, 2, 7, 6, 40
  markcolor  = 255, 140, 60
  shimmer    = 8, 0.6
  sound      = none
  smokevolume = 10, 0.35, 0.25
  damage     = cut, 2.5, 5, 0.7, 0.03, 0.15, travel
end

hotspot saw_cut_wood         # a chain held on wood: sawdust spraying, a fine dust cloud, splinters, friction smoke; no light
  merge      = 16
  heat       = 0.3, 1.2, 1.0
  bursts     = sawdust_spray 16, saw_dust_wood 8, splinter_wood 3, saw_smoke_wood 3
  sound      = none
  smokevolume = 12, 0.45, 0.45
  damage     = cut, 3, 6.5, 0.9, 0.08, 0, travel
end

hotspot saw_cut_dirt         # a chain held in dirt and rock: clods, a rolling dust cloud, specks off the stones in it

# ---- the same arsenal on each material. A variant REPLACES its base -- nothing is
# inherited -- so each lists everything it keeps. Liquids take no mark (a scorch
# stamped on water reads as a sticker); debris is the material's own, never
# concrete chips off wood or sparks off dirt. Plasma and the BFG throw no debris,
# so only their liquid landing differs.
impact plasma.liquid
  heat       = 20, 0.8, 10
  bursts     = plasma_splash, splash_liquid, plasma_ozone
  maybe      = plasma_motes 0.6, plasma_arcs 0.5
  vary       = count 0.35, size 0.2, light 0.25
  light      = 140, 2.5, 5
  lightcolor = 120, 180, 255
  volume     = rsb_plasma_burst
  exposure   = 0.4, 128, 0.6, 120, 180, 255
end

impact plasma_carbine     # the carbine's hit: sharper -- more splash, a smaller ring, a harder, shorter light, arcs now and then
  heat       = 14, 0.7, 8
  bursts     = plasma_splash, plasma_ozone
  maybe      = plasma_arcs 0.35, plasma_motes 0.5
  vary       = count 0.4, size 0.2, light 0.3
  scale      = 1.2, 0.85
  mark       = ring, 5, 55
  markcolor  = 150, 200, 255
  light      = 110, 2.8, 3
  lightcolor = 150, 200, 255
  damage     = scorch_ring, 6, 0.2, 0.55, 0.45
  volume     = rsb_plasma_burst
  exposure   = 0.4, 128, 0.6, 140, 200, 255
end

impact plasma_carbine.liquid
  heat       = 14, 0.7, 8
  bursts     = plasma_splash, splash_liquid, plasma_ozone
  maybe      = plasma_arcs 0.3
  vary       = count 0.4, size 0.2, light 0.3
  scale      = 1.2, 0.85
  light      = 110, 2.8, 3

  volume     = rsb_plasma_burst

# ---- LASER BURN: where a laser beam burns into a surface. First user: the Unmaker
# (WM_Unmaker), Doom 64's demon laser. Saturated red belongs to its hot and energy
# parts only (like plasma and the BFG); its smoke is lit and never glows. The beam
# itself is the weapon's; this is what it leaves behind.
burst laser_sparks         # hot red-white spatter thrown off the burn
  count   = 22
  cone    = 60
  speed   = 260, 0.5
  life    = 0.35, 0.4
  color   = 255, 110, 80
  glow    = 3.5
  size    = 0.8, 0.2
  gravity = 380
  drag    = 2.2
  orient  = streak
  stretch = 0.025
  aim     = reflect
end

burst laser_spray          # a spray of streaks, red-hot (rsb_spark_spray, tinted)
  count    = 1
  cone     = 8
  speed    = 0, 0
  life     = 0.22, 0.2
  color    = 255, 120, 90
  aim      = reflect
  offset   = 1
  particle = rsb_spark_spray
end

burst laser_droplets       # molten droplets that fall and cool
  count   = 10
  cone    = 70
  speed   = 110, 0.5
  life    = 0.8, 0.4
  color   = 255, 70, 35
  glow    = 2.8
  size    = 1.2, 0.3
  gravity = 450
  drag    = 1.0
end

burst laser_smoke          # smoke curling off the burn (rsb_soot_puff: lit, never glowing)
  count    = 3
  cone     = 30
  speed    = 30, 0.5
  life     = 1.8, 0.3
  aim      = up
  offset   = 4
  particle = rsb_soot_puff
end

# THE UNMAKER (owner: "frightening as fuck", kills that MELT). Its bolt lands 35 times a
# second on one place, so a hit only feeds its hotspot and now and then spits; the spot does
# the rest. On a live body it cooks -- steam and charred flecks, no sparks.
impact unmaker
  maybe     = unmaker_spatter 0.15
  sound     = none
  hotspot   = unmaker_burn
end

# ---------------------------------------------------------------- TRAILS
#   what a beam leaves in the air from muzzle to hit: RSB_Trail.Lay(profile, shooter, from, to)
#   line          thick, soft (map units), intensity, fade tics -- the core line (0 intensity = none)
#   linecolor     r, g, b      linecolorend  r, g, b (along the line, muzzle to hit)
#   linelook      halo, swell (how much the halo widens as it fades)
#   licks         strength fresh, strength dying (0-2), scale, speed -- the edges waver
#   helix         particle, turns per 100 map units, radius, spacing -- the corkscrew
#   helixmotion   drift (outward), life, life jitter      helixcolor r, g, b   helixmax count
#   lights        count, radius, intensity, tics          lightcolor r, g, b
#   heat          radius, strength, tics -- a shimmer along the trail
# The shot is the gun's (hitscan, damage, puff); a trail only draws. Energy weapons may
# be saturated (plasma blue, BFG green); the trail lingers and dissipates, no pulsing.

trail bfg_ray              # a BFG ray from the shooter to what it strikes: a green core in a green halo, sparks drifting off it
  line        = 0.8, 5.0, 2.0, 22
  linecolor   = 200, 255, 205
  linecolorend = 120, 255, 140
  linelook    = 0.7, 2.0
  licks       = 0.2, 1.0, 0.05, 1.5
  helix       = rsb_rail_mote, 1.0, 4.0, 6.0
  helixmotion = 6, 0.9, 0.3
  helixcolor  = 120, 255, 140
  helixmax    = 300
  lights      = 2, 120, 1.8, 8
  lightcolor  = 140, 255, 150
end

trail bfg_ray_heavy        # the Heavy BFG's rays: thicker, brighter, longer to fade
  line        = 1.1, 7.0, 2.4, 28
  linecolor   = 210, 255, 215
  linecolorend = 130, 255, 150
  linelook    = 0.75, 2.2
  licks       = 0.25, 1.1, 0.05, 1.5
  helix       = rsb_rail_mote, 1.0, 5.5, 5.0
  helixmotion = 7, 1.0, 0.3
  helixcolor  = 130, 255, 150
  helixmax    = 400
  lights      = 3, 150, 2.2, 10
  lightcolor  = 150, 255, 160
end

trail rail                 # a Quake 2 railgun: a white-hot core in a blue halo, a blue corkscrew that loosens and fades
  line        = 0.6, 3.5, 2.2, 32
  linecolor   = 205, 228, 255
  linelook    = 0.55, 2.5
  licks       = 0.1, 0.9, 0.06, 1.2
  helix       = rsb_rail_mote, 3.0, 3.0, 2.5
  helixmotion = 5, 1.3, 0.3
  helixcolor  = 80, 140, 255
  helixmax    = 600
  lights      = 4, 90, 1.6, 4
  lightcolor  = 110, 160, 255
  heat        = 6, 0.5, 20
end


# ---------------------------------------------------------------- ROUNDS
# A ROUND PAIRS TWO PROFILES, so how it flies and hits stays apart from how it
# looks -- which is what lets looks be customised per player safely.
#
#   ballistics <name>   what the GAME knows. Read exactly as written on every
#                       machine: no ~style, .material or @tier variants (refused),
#                       and no player setting reaches it.
#     speed   map units per tic (34 map units = 1 meter, 35 tics a second)
#     radius  map units
#     damage  base, dice  -> base x 1d(dice), unless the shooter sets damageMin/Max
#
#   roundlook <name>    what a PLAYER sees and hears. Style and tier variants
#                       allowed (roundlook pistol_45~cinematic, pistol_45@heavy).
#                       The menu's Round look swaps what flies (look, glide, wake)
#                       for any roundlook; impact and whiz stay the round's own.
#     look    sprite, <4-letter sprite> | none (nothing drawn in flight)
#     glide   yes | no
#     wake    <wake> | none
#     impact  <impact base> | none
#     whiz    radius (map units), sound -- a near miss past the listener's head
#
#   round <name>        what a gun names (WM_Gun.RoundProfile)
#     ballistics = <ballistics name>
#     roundlook  = <roundlook name>

# ---- ballistics
ballistics pistol_45
  speed  = 245          # about a .45 ACP
  roundmass = 324   # one loaded round, grains (7000 = 1 lb)
  radius = 2
  damage = 5, 3
end

ballistics buckshot
  speed  = 380
  roundmass = 694   # one loaded round, grains (7000 = 1 lb)
  radius = 1
  damage = 5, 3
end

ballistics rifle_556
  speed  = 900          # supersonic: it cracks past you
  roundmass = 190   # one loaded round, grains (7000 = 1 lb)
  radius = 1
  damage = 5, 3         # vanilla; a class's WM_Gun.ShotDamage overrides it
end

ballistics pistol_9mm
  speed  = 340          # 9mm: faster and lighter than the .45
  roundmass = 190   # one loaded round, grains (7000 = 1 lb)
  radius = 1.5
  damage = 5, 3
end

ballistics rifle_762
  speed  = 800          # full-power rifle
  roundmass = 370   # one loaded round, grains (7000 = 1 lb)
  radius = 1.5
  damage = 5, 3
end

ballistics revolver_357
  speed  = 400
  roundmass = 278   # one loaded round, grains (7000 = 1 lb)
  radius = 2
  damage = 5, 3
end

ballistics default         # a gun that names no round: vanilla's pistol bullet
  speed  = 245
  radius = 2
  damage = 5, 3
end

# ---- round looks
# A round look may add `heat = radius, strength, tics[, reach]`: the air the round tears
# through shimmers behind it over its last `reach` units (1024 when not given) and fades
# once it lands. It follows the round, so in slow motion the air opens up behind it.
# `tracer = every, <roundlook>`: every Nth round of a hand flies in that look instead (a
# burning tracer); its impacts and near-miss sound stay this look's. `light = radius,
# intensity` and `lightcolor`: a light the round carries in flight (a tracer lighting what
# it passes).
# `motor = <burst>`: a projectile's tail fires that burst each tic it flies. `onset = <flash>`:
# played where the projectile is when this look takes over mid-flight (an RPG's sustainer).
# `motormaybe = <burst> <chance a tic>, ...`: more tail bursts some tics (arcs round a ball).
roundlook pistol_45
  look   = model, RSBMA
  glide  = yes
  wake   = pistol45_vapour
  impact = pistol_45
  whiz   = 72, rsb/whiz
  heat   = 5, 0.45, 10
  carve  = 7, 0.85
end

roundlook buckshot
  look   = model, RSBMF
  glide  = yes
  wake   = pellet_vapour
  impact = buckshot
  whiz   = 48, rsb/whiz
  carve  = 4, 0.35
end

roundlook buckshot_magnum  # the SSGs' pellets: buckshot's look, hitting harder to the eye
  look   = model, RSBMF
  glide  = yes
  wake   = pellet_vapour_magnum
  impact = buckshot_magnum
  whiz   = 48, rsb/whiz
  carve  = 4, 0.4
end

  glide  = yes

  impact = rifle
  whiz   = 96, rsb/crack/near
  carve  = 5, 0.7
end

  glide  = yes

  whiz   = 110, rsb/crack/near

  look   = model, RSBMH
  glide  = yes
  wake   = tracer_hot_heavy
  impact = machinegun_762
  whiz   = 110, rsb/crack/near
  light  = 60, 1.4
  lightcolor = 255, 170, 90
  lightlook  = comet
  carve  = 7, 0.75
end

# The menu's Round look choices. Only look, glide and wake are used from these.
roundlook tracer         # every round a bright streak with a glowing trail
  look   = model, RSBMG
  glide  = yes
  wake   = tracer_bright
end

roundlook unseen         # nothing drawn in flight; impacts and whiz unchanged
  look   = none
  glide  = no
  wake   = none
end

# The engine's own projectiles fired as RSB_PlasmaBall, RSB_Rocket, RSB_BFGBall
# (zscript/rsb/projectiles.zs). They keep their own sprite, speed, damage and
# explosion; these looks add only the trail and the impact (in mid-air too).
# `look = none` here means "nothing added" -- the projectile still draws itself.
roundlook plasma_ball
  look   = none
  wake   = plasma_trail
  impact = plasma
  motor  = plasma_glow
  carve  = 8, 0.7
end

roundlook plasma_carbine   # the Plasma Carbine's bolt (RSB_PlasmaBallCarbine): a tighter, hotter streak, a smaller core, sharper hits
  look   = none
  wake   = plasma_streak
  impact = plasma_carbine

# ---------------------------------------------------------------- WAKES
#   perstep  particles along each tic's travel
#   spacing  map units between motes, instead of perstep: as dense at any speed or in slow motion
#   size     start, end     life  seconds     drift  map units/s
#   drag     1/s            gravity           color  r, g, b     glow
#   particle <PARTICLEDEFS definition> | none -- each mote drawn with a definition
#            (then size, gravity and drag are the definition's; color tints it)

wake pistol_vapour         # the air a pistol round tears through: a thin lit wisp that hangs (it draws the path in slow motion)
  spacing  = 6
  life     = 0.9
  drift    = 3
  particle = rsb_bullet_vapour
end

wake pellet_vapour         # buckshot: a fan of thin wisps, lighter than a pistol round's (no air shimmer: seven pellets would take every heat slot)
  spacing  = 12
  life     = 0.6
  drift    = 2
  particle = rsb_bullet_vapour
end

wake rifle_vapour          # a rifle round's thin vapour line (lit, never glowing)
  spacing  = 14
  life     = 0.5
  drift    = 2
  particle = rsb_bullet_vapour
end

# ---------------------------------------------------------------- FLASHES
#   light       radius, punch, tics      lightcolor  r, g, b
#   cone        inner, outer (degrees), length, density
#   conetics    tics
#   bursts      burst names | none
#   flame       sprite scale (0 = none)
#   smoke       count, scale, alpha
#   smokeparticle  <PARTICLEDEFS definition> | none -- GPU smoke (count x 2 puffs)
#                  instead of sprite puffs; scale and alpha then unused
#   heat        radius, length, strength, tics -- heat shimmer out of the muzzle
#   vary        light <0..0.9>, flame <..>, cone <..>, sparks <..>, smoke <..> -- each shot
#               that part comes out up to this much bigger or smaller (hashed per shot)
#   sparkvary   lean <degrees>, spread <0..0.9>, speed <..>, size <..>, glow <..>, life <..>, mix <..> --
#               EACH SHOT ITS OWN SPRAY: the spark bursts lean up to that far off the bore toward a hashed
#               side and their spread, speed, size, glow and life vary per shot; mix varies each spark
#               burst's share. Default lean 12, spread 0.45, speed 0.45, size 0.4, glow 0.35, life 0.3, mix 0.7
#   maybe       <burst> <chance 0..1>, ... -- bursts only some shots throw
#   surge       chance, scale -- now and then a bigger shot: all of it x scale
#   flameroll   yes | no -- the flame sprite turns to a new angle each shot (yes)
#   barrelheat  heat a shot, heat lost a second, smokes from -- THE SMOKING BARREL: after a
#               quick string of shots the barrel smokes, hardest at heat 1, until it cools.
#               Needs the gun mod's RSB_Barrel.Shot and RSB_Barrel.Muzzle calls (barrel.zs).
#   barrelsmoke <PARTICLEDEFS definition>, puffs a tic at full heat -- the ribbon off the muzzle
#   barrelshimmer  radius, strength -- the hot air bending over the barrel
#   groundkick  <impact profile>, reach -- a big gun's blast raises the floor under the muzzle:
#               that impact, by the floor's surface, when the floor is within reach below;
#               an optional third value is how far along the bore it lands (24; negative behind)
#   backheat    radius, length, strength, tics, offset -- hot air out of the rear of the tube,
#               starting offset behind the muzzle (a backblast)
#   sizecvar    <cvar> -- the gun's size slider: every offset along the bore (burst offsets,
#               backheat, ground kick) is multiplied by it
#   chargebursts <burst> <times a second at full charge>, ... -- gathering at the muzzle every
#               tic the gun charges (RSB_Barrel.Charge from the gun mod, with how far along)
#   chargelight radius, intensity      chargelightcolor r, g, b -- swelling as it charges
#   chargeshimmer radius, strength -- the air bending at the muzzle as it charges
#   exhaust     <burst> <times a second at full throttle>, ... -- out of an engine's exhaust port
#               every tic the motor runs (RSB_Exhaust.Run from the gun mod, with the throttle)
#   exhaustidle the share of those rates at idle, 0-1 (0.35 when not given)
#   exhaustblip <burst>, rise -- once when the throttle snaps open by `rise`, or the engine catches
#   exhaustsmoke radius, amount, heat, speed -- smoke into the room from the port, by throttle
#   exhaustshimmer radius, strength -- hot air at the port, by throttle
#   barrelglow  from heat, radius, intensity -- a barrel run hot glows: a dull light at the
#               muzzle past that heat (full auto, long bursts); barrelglowcolor r, g, b
#   throb       period (tics), depth (0..1) -- light and cone swell and ease on a beat: a gun
#               firing every tic reads as one pulsing glow, not a strobe
#
# Every gun the rig flashes gets a flash profile, bullets or not: plasma, rocket,
# bfg and rail have their own light and sparks, and `none` is no flash at all.

flash pistol
  light      = 180, 3.0, 2
  lightcolor = 255, 214, 150
  cone       = 20, 55, 80, 5
  conetics   = 3
  bursts     = flash_core, flash_sparks
  flame      = 0.05
  smoke      = 1, 0.02, 0.3
  smokeparticle = rsb_smoke_gun
  tail       = rsb/tail/pistol, 0.7
  powderburn = 28, 4, 0.45
  blastkick  = kick_puff, 40, 2
  powdervary = 0.45
  maybe      = powder_clump 0.15
  volume     = rsb_flash_star
end

# ---------------------------------------------------------------- RECOIL (GAMEPLAY)
# A gun's kick turns where its rounds go (recoil.zs; _staged/RECOIL_PLAN.md, approved). The
# weapon sheet names one: `recoilprofile`, `altrecoilprofile`. GAMEPLAY: base names only, no
# variants. The server cvar sv_rsb_recoil turns it off for everyone. First guesses, tune in the headset.
#   climb   degrees up a shot
#   drift   degrees, period in shots -- a fixed left-right walk by shot number, no RNG (period 2 never drifts)
#   recover degrees a second, delay tics after the last shot
#   max     pitch degrees, yaw degrees -- the kick's cap
#   bloom   extra spread degrees per degree of kick
#   brace   crouched multiplier, standing-still multiplier
#   view    back units, rise degrees, roll degrees, tics -- the per-shot jolt the arm and prop show (render only)
# Plasma, BFG and chainsaws have none (the owner).

recoil pistol_9mm          # the Pistolet: snappy; aimed fire is dead on, only a mag dump climbs
  climb   = 1.62
  shot    = 9.53, 3.2, 0.681
  drift   = 0.3, 4
  recover = 12, 6
  max     = 6.6, 2
  bloom   = 0.05
  brace   = 0.7, 0.85
  view    = 1.2, 6, 1.5, 7
end

recoil pistol_45           # the M4A3: a bigger push; fast follow-ups walk up
  climb   = 1.93
  shot    = 11.34, 5.4, 0.952
  drift   = 0.4, 4
  recover = 12, 6
  max     = 7.2, 2.5
  bloom   = 0.06
  brace   = 0.7, 0.85
  view    = 1.6, 9, 2, 9
end

recoil shotgun_m37         # fired as fast as it pumps it climbs; paced, never
  climb   = 3.44
  shot    = 16.03, 26.0, 3.239
  drift   = 0.8, 3
  recover = 6, 4
  max     = 9.0, 3
  bloom   = 0.1
  brace   = 0.75, 0.9
  view    = 3.5, 14, 3, 14
end

# ---------------------------------------------------------------- THE PISTOLET AT EXTREME
# Things the showpiece does ONLY at the top effects level, on top of the level's own ladder
# (which already makes everything more, brighter and bigger): `<profile>@extreme`. Every
# material a hit can take has its own, because a material variant outranks a tier one.
# Looks only: the round's ballistics and the brass's flight are untouched.

flash unmaker              # THE UNMAKER, THIRTY-FIVE SHOTS A SECOND. The owner, looking at the old one: "right now it is eye-assault" -- and it was, because every number here was written as if you fired it once.
                           #
                           # A 200-unit light for TWO tics at 35 shots a second never goes out: shot two arrives
                           # before shot one has faded, so what should be a strobe is a solid red glare welded to
                           # the screen. A 110-unit lit cone the same. Three glowing streaks a shot is a hundred
                           # and five a second.
                           #
                           # So the muzzle is now almost nothing and the BEAM is the weapon: one tic of light, a
                           # stub of a cone, one core streak. The skull still runs red-hot, because the barrel glow
                           # is steady rather than per-shot and was never part of the problem.
  light      = 84, 2.2, 1
  lightcolor = 255, 44, 26
  cone       = 12, 34, 44, 7
  conetics   = 1
  bursts     = unmaker_core
  maybe      = unmaker_arcs 0.18, laser_spray 0.05
  flame      = 0
  throb      = 7, 0.35
  vary       = light 0.08, cone 0.08, sparks 0.4
  barrelheat = 0.03, 0.4, 0.1
  barrelshimmer = 4, 0.6
  barrelglow = 0.3, 34, 1.6
  barrelglowcolor = 255, 40, 20
end

# ---------------------------------------------------------------- FLAMES
#   reach        map units the stream carries
#   speed        the fuel's speed, map units per second
#   spread       degrees the stream wanders
#   stream       bursts fired from the nozzle every tic. FOR THESE, a burst's
#                speed and life are MULTIPLIERS: of the fuel speed, and of the
#                time the fuel takes to reach where the stream lands (1 = it
#                reaches it). They never fly on past a surface the stream hits,
#                except for `cling`.
#   cling        seconds a stream puff drawn with a `particle` lives on past the
#                wall or floor the stream hits, sliding along it (its definition's
#                `collide = plane`); 0, the default, ends it there
#   tube         segments (0-8), thick, soft, stream share: a drawn-line core from the
#                nozzle to where it lands (the menu's Flame core). While it draws, the
#                stream bursts keep that share of their particles
#   tubelook     intensity, swell (the far end's halo over the nozzle's), halo
#   tubecolors   r, g, b stops, 2 to 8, the nozzle's first, spread evenly along it;
#                put the fade in the colours (a line's intensity is not blended)
#   tubelicks    strength (0-2), scale (noise cells per map unit), speed (rising)
#   heat         radius at the nozzle, radius at the far end, strength, noise scale,
#                rise: heat shimmer along the stream, riding the owner's hand
#   heatland     radius, strength: a ball of heat where it lands
#   landing      bursts where it lands (ordinary bursts), every landingtics
#   scorch       shape, radius, life (tics)   scorchcolor r, g, b   scorchtics
#   light        radius, intensity, flicker 0..1 (at the nozzle)   lightcolor
#   landlight    radius, intensity (where it lands, same colour)
#   sounds       loop, start, stop
#   pilot        bursts for an idle pilot light
#   jets         count, spacing (map units), splay (degrees): side-by-side jets
#   sputter      fuel share, sound: below that share of fuel (Stream's fuelShare)
#                the jet coughs, and the sound plays as it catches again
#   flameout     bursts when the trigger lets go (not from an empty canister)
# GPU particles are drawn at min(the definition's maxsize, r_gpuparticles_maxsize).
# That cvar is 8 until it is raised in Developer options (GPU particle size cap), so big
# puffs show only then.

burst flame_core
  count   = 6
  cone    = 4
  speed   = 1.0, 0.1
  life    = 0.3, 0.3
  color   = 255, 245, 200
  glow    = 5.0
  size    = 1.5, 4
  orient  = streak
  stretch = 0.02
  offset  = 0
end

# `particle = <PARTICLEDEFS definition>` on any burst: the definition gives
# colour, glow over life, size over life, gravity, drag, orient, stretch and
# spin; the burst keeps count, cone, speed, life, aim and offset. These two use
# RS_Ballistics' own PARTICLEDEFS: puffs that swell and cool white -> orange ->
# dark red. (Sizes past r_gpuparticles_maxsize, 8 by default, are capped.)
burst flame_body
  count    = 14
  cone     = 5
  speed    = 0.95, 0.15
  life     = 1.0, 0.2
  offset   = 0
  particle = rsb_flame_puff
end

  count    = 6
  cone     = 7
  speed    = 0.85, 0.2
  life     = 1.0, 0.25
  offset   = 0
  particle = rsb_flame_tip
end

# ---------------------------------------------------------------- EJECTA
#   look     sprite, <4-letter sprite>: five tumble frames A to E, lying on C; or
#            model, <casing class>: a 3D mesh (casings.zs, MODELDEF) that tumbles and lies flat
#            Some actor's states must use the sprite, or it is not loaded.
#   scale    sprite scale, or a model's size over its real size (1 = real)
#   speed    multiplier on the caller's throw speed, +/- jitter
#   bounce   floor factor, wall factor, bounces
#   gravity
#   sound    SNDINFO name | none
#   hot      tics fullbright out of the port
#   life     tics on the floor before it fades
#   portsmoke <PARTICLEDEFS definition>, puffs -- gun smoke out of the port as the case leaves
#   wisp     <PARTICLEDEFS definition>, tics -- the thin wisp a hot case trails as it flies

ejecta brass_45
  look    = model, RSB_Casing45
  scale   = 1
  speed   = 1.0, 0.2
  bounce  = 0.45, 0.3, 4
  gravity = 0.6
  sound   = rsb/casing/medium
  hot     = 12
  life    = 2100        # 60 s on the floor; the menu can only shorten it
  portsmoke = rsb_smoke_port, 3
  wisp    = rsb_casing_wisp, 16
end

# ---------------------------------------------------------------- HEAVY: EACH ROUND ITS OWN HIT
# The bullet guns' hits used to share one set of pieces per surface, only scaled. Each round now has its
# own character at every level: the 9mm crisp, the .45 a lazy thud, the chaingun's 5.56 chewing, the
# machine gun's 7.62 chunky, buckshot a pepper, magnum buckshot a heavier pepper. Guns firing the same
# shells still hit alike -- they differ at the muzzle.

end

# ---------------------------------------------------------------- ENEMY FIRE (owner 2026-09-15; enemyfire.zs)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------- END BREACH

# ---------------------------------------------------------------- THE GRENADE
# THE WORLD REACTING to a thrown grenade (RS_VR_Weapons, RSVG_Warhead calls RSB_Impact.Land with
# "frag"). THE EXPLOSION ITSELF IS NOT OURS: RSVG_Blast brings the flames, the smoke, the embers
# and the shrapnel, and the owner picked that look on purpose -- so nothing here spawns fire, a
# smoke column or a fireball volume. What it brings is what the room does about it: a crater and a
# scorch where it went off, the surface own debris thrown out of it, a dust ring rolling across the
# floor, a blast light (a sprite explosion lights nothing), the shove, the air bent over the heat,
# and the roll coming back off the walls. The bang is ours as well (owner, 2026-09-18: "yes, and
# let it use our blast sound too") -- a frag-sized blast, so the warhead plays none of its own.
# Sized between a rocket and a pistol hit: a grenade cracks and throws grit, it does not level a room.
impact frag
  heat        = 54, 1.3, 26
  bursts      = chips_concrete, slabs_concrete, rubble_concrete, dust_concrete, dust_kick_ring, spark_spray
  mark        = sunburst, 34, 150
  markcolor   = 255, 130, 60
  light       = 360, 3.6, 9
  lightcolor  = 255, 175, 110
  sound       = rsb/blast/med
  tail        = rsb/blast/roll, 0.9
  push        = 300, 760
  damage      = crater, 26, 0.6, 0.9, 0
  exposure    = 2.5, 416
  hearing     = 2.2, 384
  ripple      = 130, 0.9, 13, 0, 0.1
end

impact frag.metal   # metal: the plate rings, sparks off it, torn shards, a shallower dish
  heat        = 54, 1.3, 26
  bursts      = spark_metal, shards_metal, spark_spray, dust_kick_ring
  mark        = sunburst, 34, 150
  markcolor   = 255, 130, 60
  light       = 360, 3.6, 9
  lightcolor  = 255, 175, 110
  sound       = rsb/blast/med
  tail        = rsb/blast/roll, 0.9
  push        = 300, 760
  damage      = crater, 20, 0.35, 0.8, 0.6
  exposure    = 2.5, 416
  hearing     = 2.2, 384
  ripple      = 130, 0.9, 13, 0, 0.1
end

impact frag.wood   # wood: splinters and planks, dust off the grain, a deeper bite
  heat        = 54, 1.3, 26
  bursts      = splinter_wood, planks_wood, dust_wood, dust_kick_ring_wood, spark_spray
  mark        = sunburst, 34, 150
  markcolor   = 255, 130, 60

  sound       = rsb/blast/med
  tail        = rsb/blast/roll, 0.9

# ---------------------------------------------------------------- EXTREME: THE VANILLA ROSTER
# Every Vanilla gun at the top effects level: each keeps its own character and does MORE of it,
# on top of the level's ladder. Material variants each get their own (a material outranks a tier).
# Some bases are shared with Vanilla+ guns (buckshot, brass_45/556/762, rocket, plasma_ball, bfg_ball,
# the saw puff), so those guns take these at Extreme too. Looks only.

# ============================================================ THE HOUSE RECIPE FOR RECOIL
# EVERY OTHER KIND HAS A `default` AND RECOIL NEVER DID, and that single absence is the entire
# ballistics gap across all five weapon sets: seventeen guns with NO KICK AT ALL -- not a generic one,
# none -- because an unstated recoil profile resolves to nothing and Step returns 0, 0, 0.
#
# Not seventeen problems. One missing profile, seventeen times. The coverage table showed GENERIC at
# zero everywhere, which is the finding under the finding: every gun that fires states its own flash,
# ejecta and round, so nothing leans on a fallback for those three, and the only kind anything needed a
# default for is the one kind that did not have one.
#
# A FLOOR, NOT A DESTINATION. This exists so no gun is ever DEAD in the hands; a gun that deserves its
# own character still gets its own profile. It is deliberately modest -- about half a Pistolet -- so
# that inheriting it is never mistaken for being tuned.
#
# IT IS REACHED. Held back for one pass while the owner was playing, then he sent the decision back to
# this lane -- "ballistics is on it" -- so the seventeen kick now. `sv_rsb_recoil_default` still exists
# and still turns it off, because the old behaviour was somebody's normal for a long time.
recoil default             # the house kick: enough that a gun moves in your hands, not enough to be mistaken for tuning
  climb   = 0.60
  drift   = 0.25, 5
  recover = 14, 6
  max     = 4, 2
  bloom   = 0.04
  brace   = 0.70, 0.85
  view    = 1.0, 4, 1, 6
end

# ============================================================ DEFLECTION, AND FISTS
# THE OWNER, IN PLAY: "THE SHIELDSAW NEEDS TO SPARK N SHIT WHEN IT DEFLECTS THINGS... LIKE FIREBALLS
# AND BULLETS AND MONSTER GOO", and "I DON'T THINK THE FISTS SHOULD HAVE FUCKIN CRAZY EFFECTS, MAYBE
# JUST A TINY BIT OF DUST WHEN PUNCHING A SURFACE".
#
# WHAT IS DEFLECTED DECIDES THE LOOK, AND A MATERIAL VARIANT CANNOT DO THAT. A `.metal` variant asks
# what the SURFACE is made of; a shield in mid-air has no surface, and the interesting question is what
# hit it. So these are separate base profiles the shield NAMES, rather than one profile with variants.
#
# HOW TO PLAY ONE (RS_ShieldSaw, one line at the moment of the deflect):
#     RSB_Impact.Land(shield, "deflect_energy", incomingTravel, true);
# The last argument is inAirToo -- a deflection happens in open air, and without it nothing draws.

impact deflect             # the generic turn-aside: a hard clang and a shower off the blade
  bursts     = spark_metal, spark_spray, ember_metal
  sound      = rsb/impact/metal
  light      = 60, 1.4, 3
  lightcolor = 255, 210, 150
end

impact deflect_bullet      # a round turned: a bright scrape along the blade and a ricochet away from it
  bursts     = spark_metal, spark_spray, spk_streaks_fine
  sound      = rsb/impact/metal
  light      = 70, 1.6, 3
  lightcolor = 255, 220, 170
end

impact deflect_energy      # a plasma bolt broken on the blade. It does not bounce -- it COMES APART,
                           # which is the whole reason a shield against energy reads differently
  bursts     = plasma_glow, energy_sparks_plasma, plasma_ozone, arcs_electric
  sound      = rsb/impact/electric
  light      = 140, 2.4, 4
  lightcolor = 120, 190, 255
end

impact deflect_fire        # a fireball swatted aside: it bursts, and the pieces keep burning as they go
  bursts     = flame_embers, melt_embers, flame_smoke, spark_hot
  sound      = rsb/impact/metal
  light      = 150, 2.6, 4
  lightcolor = 255, 150, 60
end

impact deflect_goo         # monster spit turned: a wet slap and droplets thrown off the edge
  bursts     = splash_slime, laser_droplets
  sound      = none
  light      = 40, 1.2, 2
  lightcolor = 140, 255, 120
end

# A FIST ON A SURFACE, AND ALMOST NOTHING ELSE. No sparks, no chips, no mark, no sound of its own --
# a punch is not a cutting tool and it was borrowing a cutting tool's impact. A little dust, and that
# is the entire effect the owner asked for.
#
# Punching a MONSTER now draws nothing at all, which is the other half of the same complaint and is
# fixed in materials.zs rather than here: a melee puff with no surface under it used to be handed a
# default surface anyway and played a full stone impact in mid-air.
impact punch
  bursts     = dust_concrete
  sound      = none
end

impact punch.metal
  bursts     = spark_hot
  sound      = none
end

impact punch.wood
  bursts     = dust_wood
  sound      = none
end

impact punch.dirt
  bursts     = dust_dirt
  sound      = none
end

impact punch.glass         # the one place a fist earns a real effect
  bursts     = glint_glass
  sound      = rsb/glass
  damage     = crack, 7.0, 0.3, 0, 0
end

impact punch.liquid
  bursts     = splash_liquid
  sound      = none
end

# ---- BEGIN WW2 (tools/gen_ww2_profiles.py)
# THE WW2 SET. Twenty-six guns and a flamethrower, ten cartridges, NOTHING REUSED FROM THE
# MODERN SET (owner). DO NOT EDIT BY HAND: change tools/gen_ww2_profiles.py and run it again.
#
# Every number is derived from WMSHEET.ww2's own damage, firetics and shotspread, which are
# Brutal Wolfenstein II v0.2.5's. NOT transcribed, NOT invented, and re-run rather than
# re-tuned when the sheet moves -- it already moved once and took seven of twelve rates with it.
#
# The two poles the whole set is built between: the KAR98K, 100 damage and ZERO spread once
# every eleven tics -- the biggest flash and the biggest single cloud in the game -- and the
# PPSH-41, 20 damage seventeen and a half times a second, the smallest flash and a cone that
# dies before the next shot. If they read as one family at two rates, this failed.

ballistics ww2_9mm   # 9x19 Parabellum: light, fast, clean-ish for the period
  speed  = 330
  roundmass = 190   # one loaded round, grains (7000 = 1 lb)
  radius = 1.5
  damage = none
end

# ---- EVERY FLASH ABOVE, AGAIN, AT THE EXTREME EFFECTS LEVEL.
# Generated from the base blocks by a transformer, never hand-written: see extreme_blocks().
# The ladder already scales counts, light, size and smoke (including for an authored profile),
# so these add STRUCTURE the ladder cannot -- bursts that are not in the base recipe, chances
# pushed toward certainty, a longer cone, and a shockwave on anything big enough to earn one.

# THE FLAMMENWERFER'S RECOIL, and it is the only thing it takes from the gun side. A pressurised
# tank does not snap -- it SHOVES, steadily, for as long as the trigger is down: almost no climb,
# a wide slow wander, and a recovery long enough that it never fully settles mid-burst. Bloom is
# zero because the stream is a projectile and carries no spread to grow (WMSHEET.ww2).
recoil ww2_flame
  climb   = 0.18
  drift   = 0.95, 5
  recover = 22, 6
  max     = 5, 3
  bloom   = 0.00
  brace   = 0.6, 0.85
  view    = 1.1, 4, 1, 6
end

# ---- END WW2

# ---- BEGIN VANILLA+ EXTRAS (tools/gen_extras_profiles.py)
# THEIR OWN PROFILES FOR THE SEVEN VANILLA+ EXTRAS. DO NOT EDIT BY HAND: change
# tools/gen_extras_profiles.py and run it again.
#
# These seven shared the HOUSE RECIPE for their looks as well as their kick, so a Tec-9 and an
# M16 fired with the same muzzle flash and threw the same brass. Nothing was silent -- that is
# what the fallback is for -- but two guns that look identical is not a finished set.
#
# Their cards carry no damage and no spread, only firetics and an alt mode, so the ACTION is all
# the sheet gives and the action is what says what kind of gun it is. Twins are INFERRED and each
# one is named on its own profile.

# ---- END VANILLA+ EXTRAS

# THE UNMAKER'S BEAM, to the owner's own description: "a red and black pulsating cylindrical laser
# where it looks like the two are fighting for dominance, some smoke down the laser beam, some sparks
# or fog".
#
# CYLINDRICAL MEANS STRAIGHT, so the licks are almost nothing -- a jagged beam is lightning and this is
# not lightning. The fight is between the ends rather than along a jag: hellish red at the muzzle
# bleeding to near-black at the far end, and `linepulse` trading them -- as the red core dims, the dark
# end's halo swells to take the space. Seven tics is a slow enough beat to read as breathing rather
# than flicker at thirty-five shots a second.
#
# THE SMOKE IS DOWN THE BEAM, not at the muzzle: a loose helix of soot hugging it, barely turning, so
# it reads as the air itself burning along the line rather than as a corkscrew.
#
# LAID BY THE GUN, one line per shot, the same as the rail and the Tesla:
#     RSB_Trail.Lay("unmaker", shooter, muzzlePos, hitPos);
trail unmaker
  line         = 2.2, 6.0, 2.6, 3
  linecolor    = 255, 38, 22
  linecolorend = 26, 6, 8
  linelook     = 0.8, 5.0
  licks        = 0.08, 0.16, 0.05, 0.6
  linepulse    = 7, 0.55, 2.4
  helix        = rsb_smoke_soot_gun, 0.4, 4.0, 26.0
  helixmotion  = 6, 1.6, 0.7
  helixcolor   = 90, 30, 26
  helixmax     = 400
  beamlight    = 120, 1.6, 3, 0.35
  lightcolor   = 255, 60, 34
  beamcolorend = 60, 12, 10
  heat         = 7, 0.6, 20
end

# ---- BEGIN HEAVY VANILLA (tools/gen_heavy_profiles.py)
# THE EIGHT HEAVY VANILLA GUNS. DO NOT EDIT BY HAND: change tools/gen_heavy_profiles.py.
#
# They have shared the house recipe since they were built -- an Unmaker and a Bolter fired with
# the same muzzle flash -- and Vanilla+ has just grown a copy of each. MOST OF THEIR FLASHES
# ALREADY EXISTED AND WERE NEVER WIRED (unmaker, bfg_9000, chainsaw_heavy, plasma_carbine,
# rocket_launcher, chaingun_556); building second ones would have been the easy mistake.
#
# What was genuinely missing is RECOIL. No energy weapon and no saw in this package has ever had
# a recoil profile, and for those there is no cartridge to derive one from -- so those numbers
# are STATED and each says what it reasons from. Inventing a cartridge for a BFG so the formula
# would run is false precision, not rigour.

  cone          = 20, 60, 130, 8
  conetics      = 3
  bursts        = flash_core, flash_petals, spk_heavy_streaks

  smoke         = 1, 0.055, 0.70
  smokeparticle = rsb_smoke_gun
  vary          = light 0.25, flame 0.2, cone 0.15, sparks 0.3, smoke 0.3

  barrelsmoke   = rsb_smoke_barrel, 2.2
  tail          = rsb/tail/br, 0.9
end

# ---- BEGIN ALIENS (tools/gen_aliens_profiles.py)
# THE TWO ICONIC ALIENS GUNS. DO NOT EDIT BY HAND: change tools/gen_aliens_profiles.py.
#
# Both fire 10x28mm CASELESS and NEITHER HAS AN EJECTA PROFILE -- caseless throws no brass,
# and that absence is deliberate. If one ever appears, someone has wired the wrong thing.
#
# Caseless also means the barrel keeps heat a brass case would have carried out of the gun.
# That is what killed the real G11, and it is the Pulse Rifle's whole visual signature here.

ballistics ae_10mm   # 10x28mm caseless: a heavy pistol-calibre round pushed hard, shared by both guns
  speed  = 470
  roundmass = 250   # one loaded round, grains (7000 = 1 lb)
  radius = 1.8
  damage = none
end

roundlook ae_10mm
  look   = model, RSBMC
  glide  = yes
  wake   = rifle_vapour
  impact = ae_10mm
  whiz   = 68, rsb/whiz
  heat   = 5, 0.4, 9
  carve  = 7, 0.85
end

round ae_10mm

  roundlook  = ae_10mm
end

# THE PARTICLE GUN. Not a laser, not a plasma bolt, and the difference is physical.
#
# A PARTICLE BEAM IS MATTER, NOT LIGHT. It fires charged particles at a fraction of light
# speed. In vacuum you would see NOTHING -- there is no beam to look at. What you see in air
# is THE AIR ITSELF: the particles strip electrons off everything they pass through and the
# ionised channel glows. So the visible line is not the weapon, it is the damage the weapon
# is doing to the atmosphere on its way past. That single fact drives every number below.
#
# IT FRAYS. A beam of like-charged particles pushes itself apart, and every metre of air
# scatters it further -- real accelerators call it blooming and it is why particle weapons
# are a short-range idea. So the licks START TIGHT AND END LOOSE: a clean aperture and a
# ragged far end, the exact opposite of the rail gun's clean slug and of the Tesla's
# uniformly jagged arc.
#
# IT DEPOSITS ALONG ITS WHOLE LENGTH, not at the end. The heat is the longest and strongest
# of any beam we ship, because the air is being cooked the entire way rather than at a point.
#
# AND THE CHANNEL STAYS CONDUCTIVE for a moment after. Ionised air is a path for current, so
# arcs jump along and off it once the beam itself is gone -- which is why the helix is arcs
# rather than motes, and why the line outlives the shot by a few tics.
#
# LAID BY THE GUN, one line per shot, the same as the rail and the Tesla:
#     RSB_Trail.Lay("cl_particlegun", shooter, muzzlePos, hitPos);
# Its shotclass must stop being RSB_PlasmaBall -- there is no projectile, and a ball you can
# sidestep is the one thing this weapon is not.
trail cl_particlegun   # the air torn open along a line: white where it is worst, violet where it fades, fraying as it goes and still crackling after
  line         = 0.9, 7.0, 3.2, 4
  linecolor    = 240, 248, 255
  linecolorend = 122, 58, 255
  linelook     = 1.2, 4.4
  # TIGHT AT THE APERTURE, RAGGED AT THE FAR END: atmospheric blooming, and the one thing
  # that makes this read as particles rather than as light.
  licks        = 0.12, 0.95, 0.07, 1.6
  linepulse    = 3, 0.42, 2.0
  # ARCS, NOT MOTES. The channel is still conductive when the beam has gone.
  helix        = rsb_arc, 3.5, 5.5, 14.0
  helixmotion  = 11, 2.2, 0.8
  helixcolor   = 190, 150, 255
  helixmax     = 700
  beamlight    = 150, 2.6, 4, 0.55
  lightcolor   = 225, 210, 255
  beamcolorend = 110, 50, 235
  # THE LONGEST, STRONGEST HEAT OF ANY BEAM HERE: the air is cooked the whole way, not at a point.
  heat         = 9, 1.0, 30
end

# ---- BEGIN BLOOD (generated by tools/gen_blood_profiles.py) ----

# A FIRE THAT RIDES WHAT IT WAS SET ON (`ride`). A hotspot has always been a PLACE -- a beam
# heats a spot on a wall and the spot stays on the wall, which is right for a saw and wrong for
# a fire. Riding, a spot fed by a shot that landed on a THING follows that thing: a burning man
# burns while he runs and carries his own light with him.
#
# ONE HIT SETS IT TO FULL and it burns down: 1.60 of heat against 0.11 a second is about
# fourteen seconds. Nothing feeds it again, which is the whole point -- the shot has left.
hotspot bl_burn            # a flare buried in something, burning it down
  ride       = yes
  merge      = 40
  heat       = 1.60, 0.11, 1.60
  bursts     = flame_body 14, flame_core 9, flame_licks 7, flame_tip 5, flame_embers 4, flame_smoke 6
  light      = 130, 2.4
  lightcolor = 255, 138, 48
  throb      = 7, 0.30
  mark       = pool, 4, 18, 8, 60
  markcolor  = 40, 30, 26
  shimmer    = 20, 1.1
  sound      = rsb/flame/loop, 0.75
  smokevolume = 20, 0.9
  damage     = scorch, 4, 15, 0.35, 0.55, 0.30
end

  tail       = rsb/tail/br, 0.7

recoil bl_lifeleech       # stated: a staff drawing life has nothing to push against
  climb   = 0.42
  drift   = 0.18, 9
  recover = 10, 4
  max     = 3.4, 2
  bloom   = 0.01
  brace   = 0.7, 0.9
  view    = 0.8, 3, 1, 4
end

# THE SIGIL. Blood's last weapon, and it should feel like the room disapproves. Slow, enormous,
# and lit from somewhere other than the muzzle.
flash bl_sigil            # a held breath and then a wave
  light      = 320, 4.2, 4
  lightcolor = 236, 84, 42
  cone       = 40, 96, 170, 7
  conetics   = 4
  bursts     = bfg_glow_heavy, bfg_wave_heavy, bfg_ozone, flash_embers
  maybe      = bfg_arcs 0.6, flame_embers_rise 0.5
  flame      = 0.24
  smoke      = 7, 0.08, 0.75
  smokeparticle = rsb_smoke_flame
  chargebursts = bfg_charge_motes 14, bfg_charge_arcs 8, bfg_charge_core 5
  chargelight = 150, 2.0
  chargelightcolor = 236, 84, 42
  tail       = rsb/tail/rpg, 1.0
end

recoil bl_sigil           # stated: it does not recoil so much as object
  climb   = 1.90
  drift   = 0.22, 3
  recover = 24, 10
  max     = 6.5, 3
  bloom   = 0.00
  brace   = 0.7, 0.9
  view    = 3.6, 12, 2, 14
end

# COLD AEROSOL: fine propellant, mostly invisible, faintly grey where it is dense. It travels
# far less than a flame because nothing is driving it but can pressure, and it FALLS -- gravity
# on the droplets is what makes it read as a liquid rather than a gas.
burst spray_mist          # the jet itself: a fine cold fan of propellant
  count    = 4
  cone     = 13
  speed    = 120, 0.45
  life     = 0.55, 0.35
  size     = 0.8, 0.4
  gravity  = 0.25
  drag     = 0.14
  color    = 214, 216, 218
  particle = rsb_smoke_gun
end

burst spray_haze          # what hangs behind it: slower, wider, and it lingers
  count    = 2
  cone     = 34
  speed    = 34, 0.6
  life     = 1.9, 0.4
  size     = 1.7, 0.5
  gravity  = 0.05
  drag     = 0.3
  color    = 206, 209, 212
  particle = rsb_smoke_gun
end

burst spray_wet           # where it lands: it wets the surface and creeps a moment
  count    = 3
  cone     = 62
  speed    = 46, 0.5
  life     = 1.1, 0.3
  size     = 1.1, 0.4
  gravity  = 0.4
  drag     = 0.22
  color    = 200, 204, 208
  particle = rsb_smoke_gun
end

# THE CAN ON ITS OWN. No light, no heat, no scorch and no sound: the gun's own firesound is the
# hiss, and a loop here would double it. `cling` is the only thing it shares with a flame --
# propellant does run down a wall. Everything else fire does is simply not stated.
flame bl_spray_inert      # an aerosol can, discharging: cold, wet, and harmless
  reach       = 150
  speed       = 420
  spread      = 3.4
  stream      = spray_mist, spray_haze
  landing     = spray_wet, spray_haze
  landingtics = 3
  cling       = 0.22
  sounds      = none, none, none
end

# AND THE SAME CAN WITH A LIT ZIPPO IN FRONT OF IT. Shorter than the Flammenwerfer and thinner
# -- a spray can is not a military flamethrower and should never read like one -- but it is the
# same cold jet with the fire riding on it, so the mist bursts stay in the stream: the unburnt
# propellant at the edges is what makes an improvised flamethrower look improvised.
flame bl_spray_lit        # the can and the lighter, which is the whole joke of the weapon
  reach       = 210
  speed       = 460
  spread      = 3.0
  stream      = flame_core, flame_body, flame_tip, spray_mist
  landing     = flame_splash, flame_lick, flame_smoke, spray_wet
  landingtics = 2
  cling       = 0.26
  tube        = 5, 0.85, 3, 0.45
  tubelook    = 1.4, 2.6, 0.55
  tubecolors  = 255, 244, 214, 255, 198, 104, 255, 124, 34, 182, 52, 10, 96, 20, 5
  tubelicks   = 0.62, 0.08, 2.0
  heat        = 4, 15, 1.0, 0.08, 26
  heatland    = 24, 1.15
  scorch      = pool, 9, 95
  scorchcolor = 255, 92, 20
  scorchtics  = 10
  light       = 190, 2.2, 0.42
  lightcolor  = 255, 148, 58
  landlight   = 130, 1.9
  sounds      = rsb/flame/loop, rsb/flame/start, rsb/flame/stop
  sputter     = 0.3, rsb/flame/hiss
end

ballistics bl_flare        # a 26.5 mm star: heavy, slow, and you watch it go
  speed  = 26
  radius = 3.0
  roundmass = 900   # one loaded round, grains (7000 = 1 lb)
  damage = none
end

# IT IS THE BRIGHTEST THING IN THE ROOM WHILE IT FLIES. A flare is a light source that happens
# to be travelling, so it gets the heavy tracer's model, a burning wake and a comet of light --
# and at 26 units a tic it is slow enough to actually watch, which is the point of the weapon.
roundlook bl_flare
  look      = model, RSBMH
  glide     = yes
  wake      = tracer_hot_heavy
  impact    = bl_flare
  whiz      = 60, rsb/whiz
  light     = 96, 2.2
  lightcolor = 255, 140, 46
  lightlook = comet
  heat      = 7, 0.7, 14
end

round bl_flare
  ballistics = bl_flare
  roundlook  = bl_flare
end

# A FLARE PISTOL DOES NOT CRACK, IT WHUMPS. A small charge behind a heavy star: a fat soft
# bloom of orange, a lot of light, and no sharp edge to it at all.
flash bl_flaregun          # the launch: soft, wide, and very orange
  bursts     = flash_core, flash_petals_wide, flash_plume_wide, flash_embers, flash_soot
  cone       = 26, 62, 120, 7
  conetics   = 3
  flame      = 0.22
  light      = 150, 2.6, 3
  lightcolor = 255, 146, 52
  smoke      = 4, 0.05, 0.55
  tail       = rsb/tail/shotgun, 0.7
end

# 2.8 ft-lb, Vg 8.14 fps, impulse 0.683 lb-s -- a 540 gr star at 250 fps out of a 2.70 lb pistol.
# Derived, not chosen: same arithmetic as every other gun here.
recoil bl_flaregun         # a slow heavy shove, not a snap
  climb   = 1.39
  shot    = 8.14, 2.8, 0.683
  drift   = 0.35, 5
  recover = 22, 6
  max     = 6.0, 3
  bloom   = 0.00
  brace   = 0.6, 0.85
  view    = 2.6, 7, 1, 8
end

# ---- END BLOOD ----

# ============================================================================================
# `none` FOR EVERY KIND THAT CAN TAKE ONE.
#
# `flash none` has been legal since the first flamethrower and nothing else ever got its twin, so
# for years the only way a gun could say "this genuinely throws no brass" was to name NO profile
# -- which is byte-identical to forgetting one, and falls back to the house recipe besides. The
# weapons lane hit the wall stating `recoilprofile = "none"` on a spray can, which the owner ruled
# should not kick at all, and their lint was right to refuse a name nothing defined.
#
# THE CODE HATCH IS WHAT ACTUALLY DECIDES. Each of these is short-circuited in ZScript before the
# registry is ever consulted (RSB_Recoil.Profile, RSB_Ejecta.Throw/Toss, RSB_Trail.Lay). These
# blocks exist so the NAME RESOLVES -- so a lint, a coverage checker or a person grepping for it
# finds a real definition and knows the silence was chosen. They are deliberately empty or zeroed,
# and if one is ever consulted anyway it still does nothing.
#
# The rule going forward: any kind a gun can NAME gets its `none` when the kind is born, not when
# somebody finally needs it.
# ============================================================================================

recoil none              # no kick at all: a spray can, a taser, anything with nothing to push back
end

# EMPTY ON PURPOSE, AND THE PARSER PROVED IT HAS TO BE. Zeroing these was the obvious first
# attempt and was refused: `scale must be above 0`, `line wants 4 values`. The parser is right --
# a casing of zero size is a broken casing, not an absent one, and it refuses to describe
# nonsense. Absence is not a value of the thing; it is the absence of the thing, and it lives in
# the CODE HATCH, which returns before the registry is ever consulted. These blocks carry the
# ordinary defaults and are never read.
ejecta none              # throws nothing: caseless ammunition, or a gun that keeps its brass
end

trail none               # draws no beam
end

# THERE IS NO `round none`, AND THAT IS A DECISION. The weapons lane asked for one alongside the
# other three, for symmetry. A round is not like a kick or a casing: those are things a shot MAY
# have, while the round IS the shot. The parser says so out loud -- `round none REFUSED -- it
# never states ballistics` -- and it is right, because a round with no ballistics is broken rather
# than quiet. A gun that fires nothing of ours simply does not name a roundprofile and does not
# spawn an RSB_Bullet; there is no silence for this key to express. Symmetry is not a reason to
# define something that would mean nothing, and a key nobody consults is the defect we keep
# finding. If a real caller ever appears -- a round that flies on its actor's own speed with no
# looks -- it gets built then, with that caller to check it against.
# ---- END NEW SETS
